subtraction, arbitrary subset outputs, unrelated denominators, adaptive
factor-dependent computation, other groups, and general rational or
arithmetic circuits remain outside.

\section{Signed aggregation separates from product-only iteration}

\claimstatus{DEF-021}{DEFINITION}. Retain a DEF-020 public factor chain and
introduce aligned public signed coefficients \(c_1,\ldots,c_r\). Compute
\[
W_i=c_iQ_i\pmod N,\qquad R=\sum_{i=1}^{r}W_i\pmod N,
\]
while retaining every DEF-020 stage and denominator output. The coefficient
encodings and reductions, \(r\) scalar multiplications, \(r-1\) additions,
all coefficient, quotient, weighted-stage, and aggregate GCDs, extraction,
and requested formal output are charged. The exact polynomial is
\[
F(X)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_iS_{A_i}(X^{M_{i-1}})
    =\sum_i\sum_{j=0}^{A_i-1}c_iX^{jM_{i-1}}.
\]
Its uncollected sparse representation has exactly \(\sum_iA_i\) term
records, its degree is at most \(\max_i(M_i-M_{i-1})\), and collection can
only reduce the number of nonzero terms. Dense and sparse outputs are charged
by their actual sizes and coefficient bit lengths.

\claimstatus{BAR-016}{PROVED}. BAR-015's product component implication does
not extend to signed addition, even when every charged component is a unit.
Take
\[
N=9,\quad g=2,\quad(A_1,A_2)=(5,5),\quad(c_1,c_2)=(-1,1).
\]
The prefix exponents are \(1,5,25\), and the two quotient residues are
\[
Q_1=S_5(2)\equiv4,\qquad Q_2=S_5(2^5)\equiv7\pmod9.
\]
Every retained prefix, numerator, composed denominator, endpoint, public
multiplier, coefficient, quotient, and weighted-stage GCD equals one.
Nevertheless
\[
R\equiv Q_2-Q_1\equiv3\pmod9,\qquad\gcd(R,9)=3.
\]
The exact collected polynomial,
\[
S_5(X^5)-S_5(X)
=-X-X^2-X^3-X^4+X^5+X^{10}+X^{15}+X^{20},
\]
has eight explicitly charged nonzero monomials.

\claimstatus{REF-017}{REFUTED}. A proper signed aggregate need not be
accompanied by a proper charged component GCD. This existence counterexample
is not a universal schedule, success-rate theorem, or lower bound for
adaptive, multiple-aggregate, or general arithmetic circuits.
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-016} gives the complete exact audit.

\section{A symmetric signed-difference reduction}

\claimstatus{DEF-022}{DEFINITION}. Restrict DEF-021 to depth two with
\((A_1,A_2)=(A,A)\), \(A\ge2\), and coefficients \((-1,1)\). Its exact
polynomial and modular value are
\[
D_A(X)=S_A(X^A)-S_A(X),\qquad D_A(g)\bmod N.
\]
Charge the endpoint \(g^{A-1}-1\), the cofactor defined below, their GCDs,
and any requested expanded output in addition to the retained DEF-021 paths.

\claimstatus{BAR-017}{PROVED}. The exact factorization
\[
D_A(X)=X\bigl(X^{A-1}-1\bigr)H_A(X)
\]
holds, where
\[
H_A(X)=\sum_{j=1}^{A-1}X^{j-1}S_j(X^{A-1})
      =\sum_{j=1}^{A-1}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}
        X^{j-1+(A-1)k}.
\]
The cofactor has \(A(A-1)/2\) distinct positive monomials and degree
\(A(A-2)\). If \(p^e\mid N\) and \(p\nmid g\), put
\[
\alpha=\min\{e,v_p(g^{A-1}-1)\},\qquad
\beta=\min\{e,v_p(H_A(g))\}.
\]
Then
\[
\min\{e,v_p(D_A(g))\}=\min\{e,\alpha+\beta\}.
\]
Consequently, if the endpoint GCD is one, the difference and cofactor GCDs
are equal and unit division recovers the cofactor residue; if the endpoint
GCD is proper, the endpoint already factors \(N\); and if it is \(N\), the
difference GCD is also \(N\).

The cofactor itself is compactly evaluable. With \(x=g\bmod N\),
\(y=x^{A-1}\bmod N\), \(t_j=x^{j-1}S_j(y)\), and
\(h_j=\sum_{i=1}^{j}t_i\), the state
\((t_j,z_j,h_j)^\mathsf{T}\), \(z_j=(xy)^j\), starts at
\((1,xy,1)^\mathsf{T}\) and advances under
\[
\begin{pmatrix}
x&1&0\\
0&xy&0\\
x&1&1
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Binary matrix powering therefore evaluates \(H_A(g)=h_{A-1}\) with
\(O(\log A)\) modular matrix multiplications after the endpoint power.

\claimstatus{REF-018}{REFUTED}. The M21 witness is not a cofactor-only
phenomenon: for \(N=9,g=2,A=5\),
\(\gcd(2^4-1,9)=3\). The cofactor branch is nevertheless genuine. For
\(N=55,g=2,A=3\), the endpoint \(2^2-1=3\) is a unit, while
\[
H_3(2)=1+2+2^3=11,\qquad \gcd(D_3(2),55)=11.
\]
This exact classification is restricted to equal depth-two factors and
coefficients \((-1,1)\); it is neither a universal schedule nor a reduction
for unequal factors or general signed forms. Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-017}
gives the complete proof.

\section{An unequal signed-form reduction}

\claimstatus{DEF-023}{DEFINITION}. Let public \(A,B\ge2\) be unequal, let
public \(c_1,c_2\ne0\), and define
\[
Q_1(X)=S_A(X),\quad Q_2(X)=S_B(X^A),\quad
F(X)=c_1Q_1(X)+c_2Q_2(X).
\]
For the normalized difference put
\[
D_{A,B}(X)=Q_2(X)-Q_1(X),\qquad h=\gcd(A-1,B-1).
\]
Charge both geometric-sum evaluations, coefficient reductions, every
retained residue and GCD, any unit inversion, extraction, and requested
formal output.

\claimstatus{BAR-018}{PROVED}. The stage polynomials satisfy
\[
Q_2(X)-B=(X-1)Q_1(X)\sum_{j=1}^{B-1}S_j(X^A).
\]
Hence they are coprime over \(\mathbb Q[X]\), their resultant is
\(B^{A-1}\), and every integer common stage divisor divides \(B\).
For \(L=Q_1(g)\), the signed aggregate has a total trichotomy. If \(L\) is a
unit, then
\[
\gcd(F(g),N)
=\gcd\!\left(c_1+c_2Q_2(g)L^{-1},N\right).
\]
A proper \(L\)-GCD already factors \(N\). A full \(L\)-collision forces
\(g^A=1\), \(Q_2(g)=B\), and \(F(g)=c_2B\pmod N\).

There is an integer polynomial \(C_{A,B}\) such that
\[
D_{A,B}(X)=XS_h(X)C_{A,B}(X),
\]
and the two natural endpoint GCDs are exact:
\[
\gcd_{\mathbb Q[X]}(D_{A,B},X^{A-1}-1)
=\gcd_{\mathbb Q[X]}(D_{A,B},X^{B-1}-1)=S_h(X).
\]
Thus local valuations split between \(S_h(g)\) and \(C_{A,B}(g)\), and the
common factor has total unit, proper, or full semantics.

\claimstatus{REF-019}{REFUTED}. The common factor is not a complete
explanation. At \((N,g,A,B)=(25,3,3,2)\), both stages \(13,3\) are units,
the common factor is the unit \(3\), but
\[
D_{3,2}(3)=15,\qquad \gcd(15,25)=5.
\]
Unit division and the rational-prefix reduction both return \(5\bmod25\).
Conversely, \((N,g,A,B)=(9,2,5,7)\) exercises the proper common-factor
path. This classification leaves the surviving unit-prefix rational
residue open and makes no universal schedule or general factoring claim.
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-018} gives the complete proof.

\section{Primitive rational residues and exceptional cyclotomic factors}

\claimstatus{DEF-024}{DEFINITION}. Write
\[
d=\gcd(|c_1|,|c_2|),\qquad a_i=c_i/d,\qquad
P(X)=a_1Q_1(X)+a_2Q_2(X),
\]
so \(F=dP\) and \((a_1,a_2)\) is primitive. Retain the content trichotomy,
the primitive aggregate, both stage-overlap GCDs, the unit-prefix rational
residues, and compact resultant descriptors. Exact cyclotomic searches have
an explicit charged order bound; expanded integers and polynomials are
charged by their actual output size.

\claimstatus{BAR-019}{PROVED}. Content gives a total unit, proper, or full
exit. On the unit-content branch,
\(\gcd(F(g),N)=\gcd(P(g),N)\). The exact stage resultants are
\[
\operatorname{Res}(Q_1,F)=(c_2B)^{A-1},\qquad
\operatorname{Res}(Q_2,F)=c_1^{A(B-1)}B^{A-1}.
\]
More directly,
\[
\gcd(Q_1(g),F(g),N)\mid\gcd(c_2B,N),\qquad
\gcd(Q_2(g),F(g),N)\mid\gcd(c_1B,N).
\]
Thus every aggregate overlap with a charged stage is controlled by a public
coefficient--multiplier GCD. For a root of unity away from the stage
denominators,
\[
F(\zeta)=0
\iff c_1(\zeta^A-1)^2+
c_2(\zeta-1)(\zeta^{AB}-1)=0.
\]
This is an exact criterion but not a uniform classification of exceptional
orders.

\claimstatus{REF-020}{REFUTED}. The natural boundary and common-step factors
do not exhaust the primitive cyclotomic possibilities. For
\((A,B,c_1,c_2)=(3,7,1,1)\),
\[
Q_1(i)=i,\qquad Q_2(i)=-i,
\]
so \(\Phi_4(X)=X^2+1\) divides \(F\). At
\((N,g)=(55,2)\), the stages are \(7,8\), both public overlap bounds and the
content are units, but
\[
F(2)=15\pmod {55},\qquad \gcd(15,55)=5.
\]
The unit-prefix rational residue is \(10\) and has the same GCD. Resultants
therefore isolate stage overlap without absorbing the exceptional
cyclotomic residue. Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-019} gives the complete proof.

\section{A complete rational root-orbit classification}

\claimstatus{DEF-025}{DEFINITION}. For unequal public \(A,B\ge2\), let
\(\zeta\) be primitive of order \(n>1\), outside the zero sets of both
stages, and define
\[
R_{A,B}(\zeta)=-\frac{S_B(\zeta^A)}{S_A(\zeta)}.
\]
Rationality means that every Galois conjugate has the same value. Equivalently,
the remainders of the two stage polynomials modulo \(\Phi_n\) are rationally
proportional. The compact all-order descriptor retains
\(h=\gcd(A-1,B-1)\) and two fixed congruence flags; divisor lists and expanded
polynomials are charged by their actual construction and output.

\claimstatus{THM-003}{PROVED}. The ratio is rational exactly in the following
three cases:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{order condition} & R_{A,B}(\zeta) & (c_1,c_2)\\
\hline
n\mid\gcd(A-1,B-1) & -1 & (-1,1)\\
n=4,\ A\equiv B\equiv3\pmod4 & 1 & (1,1)\\
n=6,\ A\equiv5,\ B\equiv3\pmod6 & 2 & (2,1).
\end{array}
\]
The coefficient pair is primitive and canonical up to simultaneous sign.
Given \(A,B,n\) in binary, recognition needs only comparisons, modular
reductions, and GCDs, hence is polynomial in their bit lengths. Given only
\(A,B\), all common-step orders are represented compactly as the divisors
greater than one of \(h\), plus the enabled fixed orders four and six.

For the proof, rationality and complex conjugation first force
\[
n\mid A(B-2)+1.
\]
If \(bA\equiv1\pmod n\) and \(k\equiv b-1\pmod n\), Galois invariance gives
\[
R_{A,B}(\zeta)+1
=\left|\frac{1-\zeta^k}{1-\zeta}\right|^2
=\frac{\sin^2(k\pi/n)}{\sin^2(\pi/n)}.
\]
This is a rational algebraic integer, hence a nonnegative integer. Its
cyclotomic field norm leaves only the three displayed cases. Appendix
\ref{proof:THM-003} supplies every norm case and the converses.

\claimstatus{REF-021}{REFUTED}. The phase congruence alone is not sufficient.
For \((A,B,n)=(2,4,5)\), it holds and neither stage vanishes, but a primitive
fifth-root embedding gives
\[
R_{2,4}(\zeta)=\frac{1+\sqrt5}{2}.
\]
Exact reduction modulo \(\Phi_5\) agrees. Square-free \(\Phi_4\), repeated
prime \(\Phi_4\), and square-free \(\Phi_6\) modular witnesses show that both
exceptional families are nonempty after the M24 stage and public-bound checks,
without implying a universal factoring schedule.

\section{Compact extraction from the exceptional cofactors}

\claimstatus{DEF-026}{DEFINITION}. In the order-four family write
\[
F_4(X)=S_A(X)+S_B(X^A)=\Phi_4(X)C_4(X),
\quad A\equiv B\equiv3\pmod4,
\]
and in the order-six family write
\[
F_6(X)=2S_A(X)+S_B(X^A)=\Phi_6(X)C_6(X),
\quad A\equiv5,\ B\equiv3\pmod6.
\]
Retain both stage GCDs, both M24 public overlap bounds, the direct
cyclotomic and independently evaluated cofactor GCDs, the aggregate,
prime-power valuations, extraction priority, and requested formal output.

\claimstatus{BAR-020}{PROVED}. Both quotients are monic integer polynomials
of degree \(A(B-1)-2\). Nevertheless, their values modulo \(N\) admit
constant-size descriptors with \(O(\log A+\log B)\) binary modular
evaluation cost and no modular division.

For \(\Phi_4\), put \(A=4a+3\), \(B=4b+3\),
\[
U_4(4t+3;x)=(1+x)S_t(x^4)+x^{4t},
\quad D_4(A;x)=S_A(-x^2),
\]
and let \(r=A-2\), \(s=A(B-2)\), \(k=(s-r)/2\). Then
\[
C_4(x)=U_4(A;x)+D_4(A;x)U_4(B;x^A)+x^rS_k(-x^2).
\]
For \(\Phi_6\), put \(A=6a+5\), \(B=6b+3\) and
\[
H=x^3+2x^2+2x+1,\qquad J=x^4+x^3-x-1.
\]
The substitution quotient
\(D_6(A;x)=\Phi_6(x^A)/\Phi_6(x)\) has two period-six coefficient
segments, so
\[
\begin{split}
C_6(x)={}&2H(x)S_{a+1}(x^6)\\
&+D_6(A;x)\bigl(H(x^A)S_b(x^{6A})+x^{6Ab}\bigr)\\
&+2x^AJ(x)S_{Ab}(x^6).
\end{split}
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-020} gives the exact segment coefficients and proves
both identities.

For every \(p^e\parallel N\), integer product valuations give
\[
\min(v_p(F_i(g)),e)=
\min(v_p(\Phi_i(g))+v_p(C_i(g)),e).
\]
Thus a proper direct or cofactor GCD extracts a factor. A unit direct
cyclotomic value makes the aggregate and cofactor GCDs equal. A full direct
collision forces the aggregate full, but the compact cofactor remains an
independent extraction path. A dense quotient request still emits
\(A(B-1)-1\) coefficients and is charged at that output size.

\claimstatus{REF-022}{REFUTED}. Direct small-cyclotomic GCDs do not exhaust
the exceptional extraction mechanism. The clean square-free witnesses are
\[
(N,g,A,B)=(15,11,3,7)\quad(\Phi_4,\ \gcd(C_4(g),N)=5)
\]
and
\[
(N,g,A,B)=(35,8,5,3)\quad(\Phi_6,\ \gcd(C_6(g),N)=5).
\]
In both, the direct cyclotomic, both stages, and both public bounds are
units. Repeated-prime witnesses are \((9,4,11,7)\), giving \(3\), and
\((25,3,5,3)\), giving \(5\). This proves genuine per-candidate extraction
power, not a public schedule, density, or general factoring theorem.

\claimstatus{EMP-025}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0025 checked 29 symbolic divisions,
61,277 compact-versus-dense cofactor evaluations and product identities,
and 122,583 capped prime-power valuations. It found 1,873 proper residual
GCDs after the direct cyclotomic, both stages, and both public bounds were
units, with zero failures and 20 selected Python/Rust/C\# agreements. This
finite box supports the implementation only; BAR-020 is proved independently.

\section{A fixed exceptional-cofactor schedule barrier}

\claimstatus{DEF-027}{DEFINITION}. For every \(p^e\parallel N\), family
\(i\in\{4,6\}\), and public unit base \(g\), define the capped local profile
\[
a_{i,p}(g)=\min(v_p(C_i(g)),e).
\]
A fixed public joint schedule is a finite set of valid tuples
\((i,A,B,g)\), chosen before \(N\), with every base, stage, public-bound,
direct-cyclotomic, compact-cofactor, aggregate, and public-resultant GCD
charged.

\claimstatus{BAR-021}{PROVED}. The cofactor GCD is proper exactly when some
local profile entry is positive and some entry is below its full exponent.
Since \(C_i\) is monic of degree \(d=A(B-1)-2\), it has at most
\(\min(d,p-1)\) unit roots modulo a prime \(p\).

The exact stage/cofactor resultants are
\[
\begin{aligned}
|\operatorname{Res}(S_A,C_4)|&=B^{A-1},&
|\operatorname{Res}(S_B(X^A),C_4)|&=B^{A-1},\\
|\operatorname{Res}(S_A,C_6)|&=B^{A-1},&
|\operatorname{Res}(S_B(X^A),C_6)|
 &=2^{A(B-1)-2}B^{A-1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus their common-root prime support is public: \(B\) for the order-four
family and \(2B\) for the order-six family. Direct/cofactor overlap is also
public. Modulo the removed quadratic,
\[
C_4\equiv u_4+v_4X,\quad
(u_4,v_4)=
\left(\frac{A(B+2)+1}{4},\frac{A(B-2)+1}{4}\right),
\]
and
\[
C_6\equiv u_6+v_6X,\quad
(u_6,v_6)=
\left(-\frac{2(A(B-2)+1)}{3},
\frac{A(B+4)+4}{3}\right).
\]
Hence
\[
R_4=|\operatorname{Res}(\Phi_4,C_4)|=u_4^2+v_4^2>0,
\qquad
R_6=|\operatorname{Res}(\Phi_6,C_6)|
=u_6^2+u_6v_6+v_6^2>0.
\]
The precheck \(\gcd(R_i,N)\) isolates every possible direct/cofactor overlap
using \(O(\log A+\log B)\)-bit integers.

Finally, fix any finite joint schedule. The product of all its positive
charged integer values has finite prime support. Choosing two distinct primes
outside that support gives a square-free semiprime on which every charged GCD
is one. Infinitely many such pairs remain. Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-021}
proves the resultants and the schedule quantifiers. The argument fixes the
schedule before \(N\); it does not apply to an input-length-indexed,
\(N\)-dependent, or adaptive schedule.

\claimstatus{REF-023}{REFUTED}. No fixed finite public joint exceptional
schedule, even with every M27 overlap precheck, factors every composite.
For the tested prefix \(g=2,\ldots,17\), minimized registered witnesses are
\[
2491=47\cdot53\quad(\Phi_4,A=3,B=7)
\]
and
\[
1537=29\cdot53\quad(\Phi_6,A=5,B=3).
\]
The infinite refutation is the finite-product proof, not this finite search.

\claimstatus{EMP-026}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0026 checked 29 exact remainders and
resultants, 30,015 unit-residue root trials, 90,045 overlap implications,
34,104 compact/dense prime-power identities and capped valuations, ten
fixed-prefix searches, and 24 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons, with
zero failures. These observations imply no density, recognizer, or
length-indexed schedule result.

\section{A length-indexed materialized-support barrier}

\claimstatus{DEF-028}{DEFINITION}. For every input length \(m\), a public
constructor that receives neither \(N\) nor a factor selects a finite schedule
\(\mathcal T_m\). The compact ledger charges binary tuple construction and
every modular operation, output, GCD, and extraction. The materialized-lift
ledger additionally emits every nonzero exact integer \(z_{m,j}\) whose
reduction is sent to a GCD and charges
\[
 W_m=\sum_j\operatorname{bitlength}(|z_{m,j}|).
\]
An exact zero is omitted because its GCD with every \(N\) is the full
collision \(N\).

\claimstatus{BAR-022}{PROVED}. Define
\[
 \mathcal P_m=\{p\text{ prime}:2^{m-1}\le p^2<2^m\},\quad
 s_m=|\mathcal P_m|,\quad
 b_m=\left\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\right\rfloor.
\]
Every distinct pair in \(\mathcal P_m\) has product of input length \(m\).
Let \(H_m\) contain the population primes dividing at least one materialized
lift and write \(h_m=|H_m|\). Then exactly
\[
 \binom{s_m-h_m}{2}
\]
population pairs make every materialized GCD one. At most
\[
 \binom{s_m}{2}-\binom{s_m-h_m}{2}
\]
pairs can even be touched, before full collisions are removed. Moreover,
\[
 b_mh_m\le W_m.
\]
Indeed, the square-free product of the \(h_m\) hit primes divides the product
of all \(|z_{m,j}|\), and each hit prime is at least \(2^{b_m}\). Universal
coverage of all population pairs therefore requires
\[
 h_m\ge s_m-1,\qquad W_m\ge b_m(s_m-1).
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-022} proves the exact quantifiers. No asymptotic lower
bound for \(s_m\) is used or claimed.

\claimstatus{REF-024}{REFUTED}. Polynomial compact cost does not imply a
polynomial materialized-lift budget. For \(t\ge2\), the valid order-four tuple
\[
 A=3,\qquad B=2^t+3,\qquad g=2
\]
has \(O(t)\)-bit public parameters and a compact binary geometric-sum
evaluator, but
\[
 5C_4(2)=7+\frac{8^B-1}{7}
\]
and hence
\[
 \operatorname{bitlength}(C_4(2))
 \ge3B-5=3\cdot2^t+4.
\]
This proves an exact compact/materialized separation, not a lower bound on
the number of distinct prime divisors or a factoring success theorem.

\claimstatus{EMP-027}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0027 checked 91 balanced primes, 623
pair lengths, 2,494 support profiles and materialized inequalities, 751,072
pair/value GCDs, 182,523 forced-unit pairs, 13 exact compact-gap cofactors,
52 compact residues, and 24 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons, with zero
failures. At level 14, 19 public-integer encoding bits described a
49,156-bit exact cofactor. These finite data imply no asymptotic population
or prime-support density.

\section{Compact cofactor support and the single-coordinate limit}

\claimstatus{DEF-029}{DEFINITION}. For \(m\ge2\), let \(C_m=C_4(2)\) for
the public tuple
\[
A=3,\qquad B_m=2^m+3,\qquad g=2.
\]
For a finite odd-prime population \(\mathcal P\), define the analytical
support
\[
H_m(\mathcal P)=\{p\in\mathcal P:p\mid C_m\}.
\]
The public algorithm evaluates only the existing compact residue
\(C_m\bmod N\). It receives neither the unknown factors nor this support
set.

\claimstatus{BAR-023}{PROVED}. Put \(E_m=3\cdot2^m+5\). Then
\[
C_m=\frac{16(2^{E_m}+3)}{35},\qquad
v_2(C_m)=4,\qquad 3\nmid C_m.
\]
The quotient primes satisfy
\[
5\mid C_m\Longleftrightarrow m\equiv2\pmod4,\qquad
7\mid C_m\Longleftrightarrow m\equiv2\pmod3.
\]
For every prime \(p>7\),
\[
p\mid C_m\Longleftrightarrow2^{E_m}\equiv-3\pmod p.
\]
Moreover,
\[
\gcd(C_m,C_{m+1})=16.
\]
For any \(s\)-prime population with \(h=|H_m(\mathcal P)|\), the single
cofactor GCD has exactly
\[
h(s-h)
\]
proper pair outcomes, \(\binom h2\) full collisions, and
\(\binom{s-h}{2}\) unit outcomes. Since
\[
h(s-h)\le\left\lfloor\frac{s^2}{4}\right\rfloor
<\binom{s}{2}\quad(s\ge3),
\]
one compact cofactor cannot factor every square-free pair in a population of
at least three primes. Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-023} gives the complete proof,
including the denominator exceptions and the consecutive-support argument.

\claimstatus{REF-025}{REFUTED}. Exponential exact magnitude or accumulated
support across levels does not certify universal extraction by this one GCD.
Consecutive odd supports are disjoint, and at a fixed level every pair on
the same side of the support cut fails: hit--hit pairs collide fully and
miss--miss pairs are units. This does not preclude several candidates from
creating a richer signature vector.

\claimstatus{EMP-028}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0028 checked 52,026 prime/level
profiles, 51,934 generic congruences, 92 small-prime quotient cases, 49,742
consecutive-support cases, 13 exact closed forms and consecutive GCDs,
82,019 balanced primes at 32 input lengths, 2,034 explicit balanced pairs,
three proper/full/unit witnesses, and 34 selected Python/Rust/C\#
comparisons, with zero failures. No same-index balanced support hit occurred
through input length 40. This last observation is finite evidence, not an
asymptotic zero-support theorem.

\section{Support signatures: separation, collisions, and candidate lower bounds}

\claimstatus{DEF-030}{DEFINITION}. At input length \(m\), a
factorization-independent constructor fixes a finite ordered list of compact
exceptional-cofactor descriptors before the particular \(N\) is received.
If their nonzero exact lifts are \(z_{m,1},\ldots,z_{m,r}\), define the
analytical signature of a population prime by
\[
\sigma_m(p)=
\bigl(\mathbf1_{p\mid z_{m,1}},\ldots,
      \mathbf1_{p\mid z_{m,r}}\bigr)\in\{0,1\}^r.
\]
The compact ledger charges every descriptor, modular evaluator, GCD, output,
and extraction. The signature is a proof object: the public algorithm
receives neither \(p\), the factorization of an exact lift, nor a support
oracle.

\claimstatus{BAR-024}{PROVED}. Let \(n_u\) be the number of primes with
signature \(u\). On square-free \(N=pq\), candidate \(j\) gives a proper GCD
exactly when the \(j\)-th signature bits differ. Hence the list succeeds
exactly when \(\sigma_m(p)\ne\sigma_m(q)\), and
\[
F_m=\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^r}\binom{n_u}{2}
\]
is the exact failed-pair count. Universal pair separation is therefore
equivalent to injectivity. For a population of size \(s\),
\[
r\ge\lceil\log_2s\rceil.
\]
If every prime must have a nonzero signature, then
\[
r\ge\lceil\log_2(s+1)\rceil.
\]
More sharply, put \(t=2^r\) and \(s=bt+a\), \(0\le a<t\). The minimum
possible failed-pair count over all \(r\)-bit assignments is
\[
(t-a)\binom b2+a\binom{b+1}{2}.
\]
When zero signatures are forbidden, replace \(t\) by \(2^r-1\). Appendix
\ref{proof:BAR-024} gives the complete exchange proof and cost boundary.

The information bounds are abstractly tight if population factors may be
used to materialize each coordinate as the product of primes whose label bit
is one. That construction enumerates the unknown primes and can have
population-scale output. It is not a factorization-independent compact
exceptional schedule.

\claimstatus{REF-026}{REFUTED}. Union coverage plus the numerical candidate
lower bound is not sufficient. On population \(\{3,5,7\}\), candidates
\((15,7)\) give packed signatures \((1,1,2)\). Every signature is nonzero and
\(r=2=\lceil\log_2(3+1)\rceil\), but 3 and 5 collide. For \(N=15\), the two
GCDs are 15 and 1.

The canonical public prefix \((C_2,\ldots,C_m)\) is a genuine polynomial
compact schedule: it uses \(m-1\) candidates and
\(O(m^2)\) total descriptor bits and modular composition steps, followed by
\(m-1\) GCDs.

\claimstatus{EMP-029}{EMPIRICAL}. The deterministic EXP-0029 audit checked
38,860 complete signature assignments, 366,284 direct pairs, 12 tight
collision minima, 82,019 balanced
primes, 2,978,644 canonical-prefix coordinates, 2,034 explicit balanced
pairs, and 34 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons, with zero failures. The
prefix was noninjective at every registered length \(9\le m\le40\). At
\(m=40\), all 22,394 primes had zero signature and all 250,734,421 pairs
collided. These are finite results for one prefix, not an asymptotic theorem
about that prefix or an obstruction to all polynomial compact schedules.

\section{Public selector family and support normalization}

\claimstatus{DEF-031}{DEFINITION}. For \(m\ge9\), before receiving \(N\),
let \(\mathcal T_m\) contain every valid exceptional descriptor
\((i,A,B,g)\) with \(2\le A,B,g\le m\). For each descriptor retain the base,
both quotient stages, both public stage-overlap bounds, direct cyclotomic,
public cyclotomic/cofactor resultant, and independently evaluated cofactor as
primitive support coordinates. Aggregate support is cyclotomic OR cofactor
support, and the remaining overlap exits are Boolean functions of these
primitive coordinates.

On the balanced population
\[
\mathcal P_m=\{p\text{ prime}:2^{m-1}\le p^2<2^m\},
\]
normalize by deleting constant support columns and merging duplicate columns.
This does not delete any public GCD: it only replaces the analytical signature
by an equivalent basis. Two primes have equal normalized signatures exactly
when every charged primitive and derived exit treats them equally.

\claimstatus{THM-004}{PROVED}. For each \(9\le m\le15\), the normalized
signature of \(\mathcal T_m\) is injective on \(\mathcal P_m\). Therefore the
public selector exposes a proper factor of every square-free
\(N=pq\) with distinct \(p,q\in\mathcal P_m\). Complete separating
certificates use respectively
\[
1,2,2,3,4,6,10
\]
normalized coordinates. These are exhaustive finite certificates for a
factor-dependent balanced promise, not an asymptotic construction or promise
recognizer. Appendix \ref{proof:THM-004-BAR-025} gives the proof and
certificate boundary.

\claimstatus{BAR-025}{PROVED}. At \(m=16\), the 270 descriptors produce
2,160 raw primitive columns. Removing 2,054 constants and merging 96
duplicates leaves ten normalized columns, but
\[
\nu_{16}(191)=\nu_{16}(227)=\nu_{16}(233).
\]
Consequently the three pair products receive only unit or full-collision
GCDs from every charged exit. An independent dense evaluator checked every
descriptor and all eight primitive exit types. This refutes only the exact
box \(2\le A,B,g\le m\).

\claimstatus{REF-027}{REFUTED}. Diversifying both exceptional families and
every parameter and base through \(m\) is not sufficient for injectivity at
all lengths. The length-16 collision is exact; larger polynomial ranges and
different selector formulas remain open.

\claimstatus{EMP-030}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0030 checked 12 input lengths, 166
balanced primes, 2,816 descriptors, 63,953 local exit profiles, 22,528 raw
and 152 normalized columns, 2,034 normalization equivalences, 705 marginal
cofactor separations, 72 Rust/C\# command comparisons, 12 profile checks, and
104 dense certificate pair checks. The selector was injective for
\(9\le m\le15\) and noninjective for \(16\le m\le20\). The canonical summary
SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{423a86409f38a4be1382e611ca94d3e2b08abfe7c1923133ab195db9c3716ae8}}.
\]
The registered certificate-vector SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{f27e1681525d9c71f488c07457ed998cd43a8ea85ccac5b6e8e1b1e7227e93d0}}.
\]

\section{Selector evaluation and the first finite thresholds}

\claimstatus{DEF-032}{DEFINITION}. For \(m\ge9\) and a public integer
\(L(m)\ge m\) fixed before receiving \(N\), let
\(\mathcal T_{m,L}\) contain every valid exceptional descriptor with
\(2\le A,B,g\le L(m)\). All eight primitive exits from DEF-031 remain
charged. If a base is nonunit, its base GCD is processed before the
unit-only continuation is skipped.

For \(L\le L'\), raw selector inclusion gives
\(\mathcal T_{m,L}\subseteq\mathcal T_{m,L'}\). A separated prime pair
therefore stays separated. Normalized column counts need not be monotone,
but the exact fixed-cap normalization equivalence transfers this raw
monotonicity to pair outcomes.

The first-injective-cap rows for \(m=16,\ldots,20\) are included in the
single integrated finite-threshold table below. They are generated from the
registered M32 schema rather than copied by hand.

\claimstatus{THM-005}{PROVED}. The public cap \(L(m)=m+11\) has an
injective signature on every complete balanced population for
\(9\le m\le20\), and hence exposes a proper factor of every distinct-prime
balanced semiprime in this finite range. Offset 11 is minimal on the range:
at \(m=20,L=30\), primes 809 and 827 still collide.

\claimstatus{BAR-026}{PROVED}. For \(L_c(m)=\lceil cm\rceil\), the
coefficients covering all five new threshold rows are exactly \(c>3/2\).
Thus \(3/2\) is an unattained infimum, not a valid smallest coefficient.
The endpoint gives \(L(20)=30\) and fails on \(\{809,827\}\);
\(c=151/100\) is one fixed public witness.

\claimstatus{REF-028}{REFUTED}. The uniform additive cap \(m+10\) does not
suffice throughout \(9\le m\le20\), by the same exact length-20 collision.

\claimstatus{EMP-031}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0031 checked 38 cap profiles,
1,206,359 local exit profiles, 283,368 raw and 1,289 normalized coordinates,
17,330 monotonicity pair checks, 3,860 normalization equivalences, and 1,930
construction-certificate pairs. Independent validation added 64 Rust/C\#
command comparisons, one nonunit-base branch, 1,930 dense construction pairs,
and 5,314 dense predecessor collision-descriptor checks. The summary
SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{5cdc44356ae8ed81d395b033e86403691205c6552bc8da3bf4414b47842463d8}},
\]
and the registered schema SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{24f506ce7cb7ad9b10f8150f064441dbc1450f7402c72a6d228a363834eb9203}}.
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:THM-005-BAR-026} proves the finite theorem and exact
threshold boundary. Neither the audit nor the proof addresses \(m>20\).

\section{Finite promise theorem and family-relative threshold synthesis}

\claimstatus{DEF-033}{DEFINITION}. When it exists, let \(L_m^\star\) be the
least cap whose DEF-032 selector is injective on \(\mathcal P_m\). On a
complete finite range through \(M\), define the common additive envelope
\(t_M^\star=\max(L_m^\star-m)\) and strict multiplicative infimum
\(c_M^\star=\max((L_m^\star-1)/m)\). These finite envelopes are not
asymptotic rates.

\paragraph{Scope of the integrated result.}
The exact object minimized is the cap \(L\) inside the public
\(\mathcal T_{m,L}\) family of DEF-032. Thus ``minimal cap,'' ``exact
threshold,'' and ``strict endpoint'' below always mean family-relative
quantities on the complete registered balanced population. THM-019 combines
the nested finite certificates to show that the cap \(m+167\) succeeds on the
finite window \(9\le m\le34\). It does not assert injectivity at \(m>34\),
recognize the balanced factor promise from \(N\), minimize over other
selectors, or establish an asymptotic cap law.

\paragraph{Integrated certificate table.}
The following table is generated from the 16 registered M31--M46 schemas by
\texttt{scripts/generate\_m50\_finite\_threshold\_summary.py}. The
\(\Delta\)-coordinate column reports a minimum incremental repair only when
that quantity was separately certified; ``n.c.'' means not separately
certified, not zero. Rows at \(m=9,\ldots,15\) sit at the DEF-032 domain floor
\(L=m\), so no in-family predecessor exists.

\input{paper/tables/finite-threshold-summary-en.tex}

\noindent\textbf{Finite-scope warning.}
This table concerns the inspected finite populations and the defined
\(\mathcal T_{m,L}\) selector family only. It is not an asymptotic rate, a
standard-complexity benchmark for general factoring, or evidence of
injectivity beyond \(m=34\). In particular, \(m+167\) and
\(c>100/17\) are finite envelopes, not bounds for \(m>34\).

\paragraph{Representative collision and repair cases.}
Four cases expose the mechanism more clearly than the full chronology.
First, at \(m=16\) the base selector leaves the initial bucket
\(\{191,227,233\}\), motivating a public cap distinct from input length.
Second, the large finite jump at \(m=28\) leaves
\(\{11867,12791\}\) through cap 103 and needs five separately certified new
coordinates at cap 104. Third,
\(L_{29}^{\star}=103<L_{28}^{\star}=104\), so thresholds on different
populations are not monotone. Finally, at \(m=34\) the predecessor cap 200
leaves exactly \(\{97927,99527\}\), and one certified new coordinate at cap
201 repairs that final pair. These cases describe finite incremental repair;
they provide no stochastic or asymptotic model for later thresholds.

\section{Polynomial numeric-cap support barrier}

\claimstatus{DEF-034}{DEFINITION}. For a DEF-032 cap \(L\), let \(W(m,L)\)
be the sum of the bit lengths of the eight positive exact primitive integers
over every descriptor. Let \(H_{m,L}\) be the balanced population primes
dividing at least one of them. This analytical ledger overcharges
continuation values skipped by nonunit-base branches and therefore only
enlarges the possible support.

Put \(b=\operatorname{bitlength}(L)\). The per-descriptor bit bounds for
base, first stage, second stage, first bound, second bound, cyclotomic,
resultant, and cofactor are respectively
\[
b,\quad Lb,\quad L^2b,\quad b,\quad b+1,\quad
2b+1,\quad4b+2,\quad L^2b+1.
\]
The geometric sums are smaller than \(g^A\) and \(g^{AB}\). The two
resultant remainder coefficients have magnitude at most \(L^2\), and the
positive exact cofactor is smaller than \(2g^{AB}\). Since there are at most
\(2(L-1)^3\) descriptors,
\[
W(m,L)\le
2(L-1)^3(2L^2b+Lb+9b+5)
=O(L^5\log L).
\tag{M47.1}
\]

\claimstatus{BAR-041}{PROVED}. Every \(p\in\mathcal P_m\) is at least
\(2^{\lfloor(m-1)/2\rfloor}\). The square-free product of all primes in
\(H_{m,L}\) divides the product of the charged exact integers, so
\[
\left\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\right\rfloor |H_{m,L}|
\le W(m,L).
\]
Rosser and Schoenfeld's inspected inequalities
\cite{rosser1962approximate} give
\[
\frac{x}{\log x}<\pi(x)\ (x\ge17),\qquad
\pi(x)<1.25506\frac{x}{\log x}\ (x>1).
\]
Because \(1.25506/\sqrt2<8/9\), applying them at
\(x=2^{m/2}\) yields
\[
|\mathcal P_m|>
\frac{2^{m/2}}{81\log(2^{m/2})}
\qquad(m\ge10).
\tag{M47.2}
\]
For every polynomially bounded numeric cap \(L(m)\), (M47.1) is polynomial in
\(m\), while (M47.2) is exponential up to a polynomial factor. Eventually at
least two balanced primes lie outside \(H_{m,L(m)}\). Both have the all-zero
primitive signature, so no derived aggregate or overlap coordinate can
separate their product. Thus no such cap is asymptotically injective.

\claimstatus{REF-043}{REFUTED}. A polynomial numeric DEF-032 cap cannot
separate every sufficiently large complete balanced population. This does
not cover polynomial-bit encodings of exponentially large numeric
parameters, adaptive or input-dependent schedules, other compact grammars,
or general factoring algorithms.

\claimstatus{EMP-046}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0046 materialized 963 descriptors and
7,704 exact primitive values at caps 9, 12, 16, and 20. Independent
reconstruction checked every value, 25,346 branch-support cases, and 31
balanced populations from lengths 10 through 40. The summary SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{b9c97e8161d3470ad61d20fd9ee8834888e5f0139e1b3fd5b3fbe3b2a6463093}},
\]
and the schema SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{a37f9d495de85943a69a6c3fbc122c9f09fbce0f78316c32163c380a782ef525}}.
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-041} gives the complete asymptotic proof.

\section{Short-span encoded compact-gap barrier}

\claimstatus{DEF-035}{DEFINITION}. For each input length \(m\), let
\(\Lambda_m=(t_{m,1},\ldots,t_{m,r_m})\) be a strictly increasing public
list of levels for the exact compact candidates
\[
A=3,\qquad B_t=2^t+3,\qquad g=2.
\]
The compact ledger charges \(O(\sum_jt_{m,j})\) modular recurrence work,
all descriptor bits, \(r_m\) GCDs, and extraction. Thus
\(\sum_jt_{m,j}=\operatorname{poly}(m)\) gives polynomial branch-total cost
even though \(B_t\) is exponentially large numerically.

Put \(E_t=3\cdot2^t+5\). If \(p>7\) divides compact cofactors at levels
\(t<u\), then BAR-023 gives
\(2^{E_t}\equiv2^{E_u}\equiv-3\pmod p\). With
\[
d=u-t,\qquad k=2^d-1,
\]
the identity \(E_u=2^dE_t-5k\) implies
\[
p\mid R_d:=3^k+32^k,\qquad
\operatorname{bitlength}(R_d)\le5(2^d-1)+1.
\tag{M48.1}
\]

\claimstatus{BAR-042}{PROVED}. Let \(J_m\) contain the balanced primes
hitting at least two coordinates, and put
\(b_m=\lfloor(m-1)/2\rfloor\). The square-free support product and
(M48.1) give
\[
|J_m|\le U_m:=
\sum_{i<j}
\left\lfloor
\frac{5(2^{t_{m,j}-t_{m,i}}-1)+1}{b_m}
\right\rfloor.
\tag{M48.2}
\]
Outside \(J_m\), only the all-zero and \(r_m\) one-hot signatures occur.
Hence
\[
|\mathcal P_m|-U_m>r_m+1
\tag{M48.3}
\]
forces a duplicate complete signature and a failed balanced semiprime.

If \(r_m\le m^a\) and the level span satisfies
\(\Delta_m\le(1/2-\varepsilon)m\) for fixed
\(\varepsilon>0\), then
\[
U_m=O\!\left(m^{2a-1}2^{(1/2-\varepsilon)m}\right).
\]
The inspected lower bound
\(|\mathcal P_m|=\Omega(2^{m/2}/m)\) makes (M48.3) hold eventually.
Therefore every such short-span compact-gap selector is noninjective.

\claimstatus{REF-044}{REFUTED}. A polynomial-size short-span encoded
compact-gap list cannot separate every sufficiently large balanced
population. Lists whose span approaches or exceeds \(m/2\), other compact
families, adaptive schedules, and general factoring remain outside the
claim.

\claimstatus{EMP-047}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0047 checked 42 shifted public
windows, 163,794 balanced-prime signatures, 1,636,992 coordinates, 11 exact
overlap integers, and three nontrivial common-support witnesses. Forty
profiles satisfy (M48.3) directly, and none is injective. The summary
SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{48b95a0e1acb799fce06e2aa25492eebc432daef6c6e8c14abd962ef0c7170d2}},
\]
and the schema SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{c1a659a6081df2b46f492379b97c9b624f0041772068d19e89337550c19eaa8e}}.
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-042} gives the complete proof.

\section{Linearly wide encoded compact-gap barrier}

\claimstatus{DEF-036}{DEFINITION}. Fix an integer \(h\ge1\). Let
\(K_{m,h}\) contain the balanced primes whose DEF-035 compact-gap signature
has Hamming weight at least \(h+1\). Outside \(K_{m,h}\), the number of
available signatures is at most
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{h}\binom{r_m}{j}.
\tag{M49.1}
\]
This high-weight set is analytical and is not supplied to the public
constructor.

Suppose one prime \(p>7\) hits
\(t_0<t_1<\cdots<t_h\). Put \(d_i=t_i-t_0\) and
\(q=\gcd(d_1,\ldots,d_h)\). The M48 pair relation says that, for
\(x=3\cdot32^{-1}\pmod p\),
\[
x^{2^{d_i}-1}=-1\pmod p
\qquad(1\le i\le h).
\]
Since
\[
\gcd_i(2^{d_i}-1)=2^q-1
\]
and every quotient
\((2^{d_i}-1)/(2^q-1)\) is odd, a parity-preserving Bezout combination
gives
\[
p\mid R_q=3^{2^q-1}+32^{2^q-1}.
\tag{M49.2}
\]
The \(h\) distinct positive offsets are multiples of \(q\), so
\[
q\le\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta_m}{h}\right\rfloor.
\tag{M49.3}
\]

\claimstatus{BAR-043}{PROVED}. With
\(b_m=\lfloor(m-1)/2\rfloor\), a union over all \((h+1)\)-subsets and
(M49.2)--(M49.3) gives
\[
|K_{m,h}|
\le U_{m,h}:=
\left\lfloor
\frac{\binom{r_m}{h+1}
\left(5(2^{\lfloor\Delta_m/h\rfloor}-1)+1\right)}
{b_m}
\right\rfloor.
\tag{M49.4}
\]
Therefore
\[
|\mathcal P_m|-U_{m,h}>
\sum_{j=0}^{h}\binom{r_m}{j}
\tag{M49.5}
\]
forces duplicate complete signatures.

If \(r_m\le m^a\), \(\Delta_m\le Cm\), and the complete compact evaluation
and GCD ledger is polynomial, choose any fixed integer \(h>2C\). Then
\[
U_{m,h}
=O\!\left(m^{a(h+1)-1}2^{(C/h)m}\right),
\]
which is exponentially smaller than
\(|\mathcal P_m|=\Omega(2^{m/2}/m)\), while (M49.1) is polynomial.
Thus (M49.5) holds eventually. Every polynomial-size, polynomial-cost public
list in this exact family with fixed linear span is noninjective.

\claimstatus{REF-045}{REFUTED}. A linearly wide encoded compact-gap list
cannot separate every sufficiently large balanced population. Superlinear
span, other compact families, adaptive schedules, and general factoring
remain outside the claim.

\claimstatus{EMP-048}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0048 checked 12 public windows with
span \(m\) or \(2m\), 62,128 balanced-prime signatures, 3,645,232
coordinates, three common-support GCD-gap witnesses, and 12 independent pair
formulas. No profile is injective and no prime reaches its registered
high-weight threshold. None of the finite profiles satisfies the conservative
inequality (M49.5), so the bounded collisions are not used as the asymptotic
proof. The summary SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{958223f09db3abd12b46fcda81cc22a553158c1cc92ef4918ad8f2bc9a3202a9}},
\]
and the schema SHA-256 is
\[
\texttt{\seqsplit{970fb769aa2d307ff691ad19ee1226bae89ad860363fb726348b8d80144239ad}}.
\]
Appendix \ref{proof:BAR-043} gives the complete proof.

\section{Compact-step and bit-operation complexity}

The mathematical results use distinct algorithmic and analytical scopes that
must not
be conflated.

\subsection{Cost units and the online/offline boundary}

Let \(\mathsf{Mod}(m)\) denote a conservative bit-operation upper bound for
one multiplication followed by reduction modulo an \(m\)-bit modulus, and
let \(\mathsf{Gcd}(m)\) denote the corresponding bound for one GCD. The
compact-step ledger counts calls to such operations; it is not itself a
bit-operation bound. Textbook arithmetic gives the sufficient bounds
\(\mathsf{Mod}(m)=O(m^2)\) and
\(\mathsf{Gcd}(m)=O(m^2)\). Faster multiplication may improve these terms,
but no tighter backend-dependent bound is required by the finite theorem.

The online public evaluator constructs descriptors from \(m\) and the public
cap, evaluates compact residues modulo \(N\), processes every failed
inversion or nonunit branch, computes the charged GCDs, and outputs/extracts
any proper factor. It receives no population list, factor, local order,
analytical signature, normalized column, or certificate. Population
enumeration, exact-lift materialization, support normalization, cap search,
dense expansion, certificate-column selection, pairwise injectivity checks,
and hash generation belong to the offline proof procedure.

For one exceptional tuple, the compact cofactor evaluator uses
\(O(\log A+\log B)\) modular operations. The stage-overlap descriptors store
only public bases and binary exponents, while \(u_i,v_i,R_i\) have
\(O(\log A+\log B)\) bits and require a constant number of integer
operations and GCDs. These are polynomial-time prechecks in the encoded
inputs. By contrast, explicit enumeration of the local root set modulo an
unknown prime costs \(p-1\) trials and is only an analytical or bounded-test
operation. BAR-021 does not treat that enumeration as a public recognizer.
BAR-022 adds a separate exact-output ledger: if exact lifts or equivalent
prime-support certificates are requested, their actual bit length is charged.
The compact M26 evaluator remains polynomial in the encoded counts and
modulus even when the corresponding exact integer is exponentially long.
Neither ledger provides the unknown local roots as public input.
BAR-023 stays on the compact ledger. The public constructor writes
\(B_m=2^m+3\) in \(O(m)\) bits and evaluates one residue with \(O(m)\)
binary-composition steps. The local congruence modulo \(p\) is an analytical
criterion, not a public factor recognizer. Support enumeration, exact-lift
factorization, and signature enumeration remain outside the public
algorithm. BAR-024 preserves that boundary for several candidates. For the
canonical prefix \(C_2,\ldots,C_m\), summing the per-level descriptor and
binary-evaluation costs gives \(O(m^2)\), while exact injectivity remains a
factor-dependent semantic property.
The DEF-031 selector contains at most \(2(m-1)^3\) descriptors. Each has
\(O(\log m)\) public encoding bits and
\(O(\log A+\log B)=O(\log m)\) compact modular composition steps. The full
selector therefore has polynomial construction, evaluation, GCD, output, and
extraction cost. Population enumeration, support normalization, and dense
certificate expansion are proof operations and are not counted as a public
recognizer.
\subsection{The DEF-032 public selector}

DEF-032 replaces \(m\) in the descriptor bound by a public numeric cap
\(L=L(m)\). The selector has
\[
D(m,L)\le2(L-1)^3
\]
descriptors. Enumerating their public fields writes
\(O(D(m,L)\log L)\) descriptor bits. Each descriptor uses
\(O(\log L)\) compact modular operations, a constant number of charged GCDs,
and \(O(m)\) output bits per retained residue or GCD result. Consequently a
conservative online bit-operation bound is
\[
O\!\left(
 D(m,L)\bigl(
   \log L\,\mathsf{Mod}(m)
   +\mathsf{Gcd}(m)
   +m+\log L
 \bigr)
\right).
\]
With textbook arithmetic this is
\[
O\!\left(L^3m^2\log L\right).
\]
For every polynomially bounded, polynomial-time computable numeric cap this
is polynomial in \(m\); for the linear caps in the finite theorem it becomes
the conservative bound \(O(m^5\log m)\). The previously used
\(O(m^3\log m)\) expression refers only to compact modular steps, not to
standard bit operations. Neither bound implies injectivity outside the
certified finite range.

BAR-041 uses a different, offline analytical ledger. It bounds the total
exact-output support budget by \(O(L^5\log L)\) to prove an asymptotic
collision for the exact numeric-cap grammar. That exact-output quantity is
not added to the online evaluator, and the conclusion relies on the numeric
bound \(A,B,g\le L\). It does not cover polynomial-bit encodings of
exponentially large parameters.

DEF-035 makes that escape explicit. At compact level \(t\), the ordinary
binary parameters and modular recurrence use \(O(t)\) bits and steps, so a
public list costs \(O(\sum_jt_{m,j})\) plus its GCDs. BAR-042 does not charge
the exponentially long exact cofactors. It instead charges their pairwise
common prime support to a gap-dependent compact overlap integer. Polynomial
cost therefore remains compatible with the theorem; short span, rather than
exact output size, is the obstruction.
DEF-036 and BAR-043 keep the same public cost ledger. They change only the
analytical charging of common support: \(h+1\) hits collapse to a single
GCD-gap overlap integer. The fixed \(h\) used for any fixed linear-span
constant adds no public factor input or asymptotic evaluation cost.

\subsection{Certificate-generation cost}

The finite theorem does not claim that its proof-generation procedure is an
online polynomial-time recognizer. The M31--M46 audits enumerate the complete
bounded prime population, search caps, recompute branch-total masks, refine
collision buckets, and in selected cases expand dense cofactors. Their
recorded operation counts and wall times are finite certificate costs. They
may be much larger than the public evaluator and are not hidden inside the
online complexity statement. The minimal publication checker verifies
frozen source hashes and table projections without rerunning those searches;
the full semantic path is documented in
\texttt{research/CERTIFICATE\_TRUST\_MODEL.md}.

\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabular}{L{0.23\linewidth}L{0.31\linewidth}L{0.36\linewidth}}
\toprule
Component & Guarantee & Complexity boundary \\
\midrule
Explicit multiplicative family &
Exact candidate semantics and recursive failure propagation &
Polynomial only if construction, cardinalities, exponent lengths, evaluation,
and recursive success are all polynomial in \(\log N\) \\
\addlinespace
Multiplication straight-line family &
Exact formal exponents and shared same-base multiplication DAGs &
\(t\) nodes use \(O(t\log(t+1))\) descriptor bits and \(t\) modular
multiplications; no fixed-modulus or richer-operation lower bound \\
\addlinespace
Addition-subtraction family &
Exact signed formal exponents after a unit precheck &
\(t\) nodes use \(O(t\log(t+1))\) descriptor bits, \(t\) multiplications,
and at most \(t\) extended-GCD inversions; no implicit-batch or
fixed-modulus lower bound \\
\addlinespace
Leaf-materialized product tree &
Exact aggregate valuation and root-versus-leaf success semantics &
\(n\) charged leaf evaluations and exactly \(n-1\) internal
multiplications; no non-materializing arithmetic-circuit lower bound \\
\addlinespace
Explicit-atom product DAG &
Exact formal multiplicities, shared subproducts, and node-versus-atom
success semantics &
\(a\) charged atom exponentiations and \(t\) product gates; unfolded
occurrences can reach \(2^t\), but richer arithmetic circuits remain outside \\
\addlinespace
Dyadic exact-division circuit &
Total denominator semantics and compact geometric-quotient evaluation &
\(t\) squarings, \(t\) explicit factors, and \(\max(0,t-1)\) products;
expanded formal output has \(2^t\) coefficients, and general rational
circuits remain outside \\
\addlinespace
Arbitrary geometric-sum circuit &
Endpoint, denominator, and public-exponent extraction trichotomy &
At most \(3(\ell-1)\) multiplications and \(2(\ell-1)\) additions for an
\(\ell\)-bit exponent; expanded output has \(M\) coefficients, and
multi-denominator circuits remain outside \\
\addlinespace
Nested geometric-quotient circuit &
Two total denominator paths and a compact cancellation certificate &
Three binary evaluators use \(O(\alpha+\beta)\) modular operations; the
quotient has \(B\) sparse monomials or \(A(B-1)+1\) dense positions, and
iterated rational chains remain outside \\
\addlinespace
Iterated geometric-quotient chain &
Explicit stage exits, linked prefix certificates, and product aggregation &
For \(L=\sum_i\operatorname{bitlength}(A_i)\), three binary evaluators per
stage use \(O(rL)\) modular operations; every requested dense, sparse, or
expanded prefix output is charged by its actual size \\
\addlinespace
Signed quotient-stage aggregate &
One explicit linear form over retained stage values; component and aggregate
GCDs can differ in extraction power &
DEF-020 costs plus \(r\) scalar multiplications and \(r-1\) additions;
coefficient encodings and every requested formal output are charged \\
\addlinespace
Symmetric quotient difference &
Endpoint/cofactor valuation classification and total extraction trichotomy &
Two geometric sums, one endpoint power, and a \(3\)-state matrix recurrence
using \(O(\log A)\) modular steps; expanded \(H_A\) has \(A(A-1)/2\)
monomials \\
\addlinespace
Primitive rational-residue audit &
Content and stage-overlap classification with exact root-of-unity criterion &
Two geometric sums and constant modular work; resultants remain compact
base--exponent descriptors, while expansions and order searches are charged
by actual output size or explicit bound \\
\addlinespace
Semismooth randomized splitter &
Complete Las Vegas factorization on the hereditary promise class &
Expected polynomial bit complexity for every fixed polynomial schedule pair
\(B,R\); no outside-promise termination claim \\
\addlinespace
Nonsplit Lucas splitter &
Exact discriminant and sequence outcomes; conjugate-map barrier; Las Vegas
factorization on the DEF-007 hereditary promise &
Binary Lucas evaluation is polynomial in \(\log N+\log d\); no
outside-promise termination or membership-recognition claim \\
\addlinespace
Reference suite &
Exact Python semantics, bounded Rust \texttt{u64}, selected C\#
\texttt{BigInteger} verification &
Validation and falsification only; no practical or asymptotic superiority
claim \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The unequal signed depth-two evaluator uses two binary geometric sums and
\(O(\log A+\log B)\) modular operations. Its collected signed form has
\(A+B-2\) or \(A+B-1\) monomials. BAR-019 adds only constant modular work
for content and overlap checks. Its compact resultants do not license
materializing integers with \(\Theta(A\log(|c_2|B))\) or comparable bits.
THM-003 recognizes a requested order using a constant number of modular
reductions and GCDs. Its compact descriptor does not license a polynomial-time
listing of all divisors of \(\gcd(A-1,B-1)\), nor free expansion of any
\(\Phi_n\) or dense signed numerator.

Binary modular exponentiation uses \(O(L(m))\) modular multiplications for an
\(L(m)\)-bit exponent. A Lucas pair can likewise be evaluated by binary
composition in \(O(\log d)\) modular operations. Every failed inversion or
nonunit precheck is an algorithmic branch that may expose a factor; it is not
silently treated as an exceptional implementation error.

For complete factorization, every reported split is verified before recursion.
A binary split tree has fewer than \(2m\) nodes because the number of prime
factors with multiplicity is at most \(m\). In the restricted theorem,
maximal-perfect-power preprocessing adds at most one unary node per subsequent
split or leaf, preserving an \(O(m)\) recursion count.

\section{Reproducible experimental methodology}

All searches are deterministic exhaustive enumerations inside declared finite
boxes; their seed is therefore recorded as none. Prime inputs, nonunit bases,
or empty families are skipped only when excluded by the tested definition.
The searches retain prime powers, repeated-prime products, Carmichael numbers,
orders \(1\) and \(2\), simultaneous collisions, discriminant degeneracy, and
explicit independently parameterized controls where relevant.

Python arbitrary-precision code is the semantic oracle. Rust independently
implements the documented \texttt{u64} overlap with \texttt{u128} modular
products, and C\# uses \texttt{BigInteger} for selected vectors. A passing
differential check establishes agreement only on those registered vectors.
The experiment records name the core commit, exact command, bounds, pruning,
toolchain manifest, and canonical summary hash.

The implementations are not described as three complete clean-room proofs.
Python produces the full finite certificates; later Python checkers use a
separately written direct mask formula but share the public descriptor
specification and selected protocol helpers. Rust and C\# independently
reconstruct selected arithmetic and branch vectors, not every late
full-population pair. The generator/verifier split, minimum trusted checker,
population reconstruction, source hashes, and residual assumptions are
specified in \texttt{research/CERTIFICATE\_TRUST\_MODEL.md}. The consolidated
M50 artifact and both table fragments are checked with:
\begin{verbatim}
python scripts/generate_m50_finite_threshold_summary.py --check
python scripts/check_m50_finite_threshold_summary.py
\end{verbatim}

The baseline suite includes modular exponentiation, trial division,
perfect-power detection, validation primality, bounded Pollard rho, Pollard
\(p-1\), a scoped Williams-style \(p+1\) stage, and per-item batch-GCD
semantics. The batch routine is not presented as a product-tree optimization.

\claimstatus{EMP-001}{EMPIRICAL}. The baseline corpus passed 58 registered
cross-language comparisons. This is finite implementation agreement.

\claimstatus{EMP-002}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0002, all 78,860 candidates agreed
with the support-sufficiency and exact valuation criteria. The search found
5,672 nonsquarefree proper GCDs not described by a proper support, beginning
with \((N,g,d)=(4,3,1)\).

\claimstatus{EMP-003}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0003, the finite oracle completely
factored all 155 hereditary promised inputs in the box, and all 557 ordered
witnesses met the proved \(5/12\) success bound. The minimum observed exact
probability was \(268/493\). This does not estimate promise-class density.

\claimstatus{EMP-004}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0004, 240 of 576 divisor covers had
noninjective signatures. The exact signature characterization, counting bound,
and control construction passed their registered checks.

\claimstatus{EMP-005}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0005, the multiplicative and combined
families both succeeded on exactly 9,037 of 9,773 conjugate families, with zero
derived-Lucas-only gains. Independently chosen parameters produced bounded
complement examples, but the tuple counts are not probabilities.

\claimstatus{EMP-006}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0006, all 1,040 exact root formulas,
714 ordered asymmetry witnesses, and 75,934 proved-event splitting parameters
passed. The minimum proved-event probability was \(8/51\), and 26 selected
Python/Rust/C\# comparisons agreed. These finite counts do not estimate
promise-class density or establish novelty.

\claimstatus{EMP-007}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0007, all 296,100 direct/signature
comparisons, 987 density and divisor-bound checks, 184,994 magnitude-zero pair
checks, and 2,443 balanced zero-density checks passed. The maximum observed
promised-pair fraction was \(49/100\), and 28 selected Python/Rust/C\#
comparisons agreed. These registered finite-distribution observations are not
a natural-density estimate, recognizer, asymptotic result, or novelty claim.

\claimstatus{EMP-008}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0008, all 262,143 exact divisor
enumerations, one-length budgets, and single-exponent hit bounds passed, as
did 2,306,048 direct prime-oracle comparisons and 987 record-family bounds.
The largest observed single-exponent hit set had 114 odd primes at
\(d=166{,}320\), and 46 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons agreed. These
finite observations do not establish budget tightness, asymptotic behavior,
compressed-representation behavior, a natural density, or novelty.

\claimstatus{EMP-009}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0009, all 1,647,202 constructed
nodes satisfied the exponent-growth bound and direct residue semantics.
Every depth through seven attained \(2^t\); all 17 tower levels through
\(s=16\) matched the exact lower bound and repeated-squaring construction;
and 24 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons agreed. These finite syntax and
residue checks do not establish an asymptotic, richer-model, density, novelty,
or factoring claim.

\claimstatus{EMP-010}{EMPIRICAL}. In EXP-0010, all 262,143 exact DEF-011
budget checks passed. Every divisor and \(d\pm1\) candidate was enumerated for
the first 12 primorials. At \(r=12\), the 43-bit exponent had 4,096 divisors,
67 binary multiplication nodes, and 897 prime hits split 449/448 between the
two disjoint channels. The within-hit promised fraction was \(449/897\), and
32 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons agreed. These finite records do not
establish asymptotic prime yield or a fraction on an external population.

\claimstatus{EMP-011}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0011 checked 9,961,434 bounded divisor
memberships, 145,413 support inequalities, 290,826 \(d\pm1\) candidates, and
16 balanced-population formulas. At \(r=18\), only 7,074 of 262,144 divisors
were at most \(2^{20}\), below the registered bound 106,762. The 32 selected
Python/Rust/C\# comparisons and four Python scale-bound records agreed. These
finite observations do not
prove the asymptotic theorem or estimate a natural prime density.

\claimstatus{EMP-012}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0012 completed 1,572,816 exact
small/large-prime split-bound checks, 4,421,736 divisor-membership checks, and
5,344,372 \(d\pm1\) prime-candidate checks with no counterexample. The 32
selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons and four Python-only exact-bound records
agreed. Prime-power, squareful smooth, noninitial square-free, and mixed
noninitial squareful records all satisfied the bound. These finite
observations do not prove the asymptotic or establish population, density,
recognition, novelty, factoring, or general lower-bound claims.

\claimstatus{EMP-013}{EMPIRICAL}. EXP-0013 completed 2,403,786 signed
node-growth checks, 190,344 direct residue checks, and 646,400 exact
positive/negative GCD comparisons. It classified 10,100 unit, 6,127 proper
nonunit, and 570 full-nonunit base prechecks; observed 734,190 negative and
251,685 zero outputs; and attained exact maxima
\(1,2,4,8,16,32,64\). All 24 selected Python/Rust/C\# comparisons agreed.
These bounded syntax, residue, and GCD checks do not prove the asymptotic or
support population, density, recognition, novelty, factoring, or
broader-model claims.

subset checks and 6,488,889 prime-power valuation-component checks. Every
one of 1,333,349 proper root GCDs had a proper leaf GCD. Among 2,183,887
batches with a proper leaf separator, 850,538 aggregated to the full

agreed. These finite observations do not prove the asymptotic transfer or
establish a non-materializing circuit, population, density, recognition,

The exact unfolded-occurrence maxima were \(2,4,8,16,32\), while 84,013

These observations audit implementations and minimize counterexamples. The
universal statements in this paper are supplied by proofs, not extrapolation
from the table.

\section{Limitations and open problems}

No universal support-POSF or valuation-family constructor is supplied.
THM-001 applies only to a factor-dependent hereditary promise and provides
neither a membership recognizer nor a density theorem. Its unbounded splitter
may not terminate outside the promise.

The computer-assisted selector theorem is also strictly finite and
family-relative. Its population is the balanced square-free semiprime promise,
its inspected window is \(9\le m\le34\), and \(L_m^\star\) minimizes only
inside the exact \(\mathcal T_{m,L}\) family. Neither \(m+167\) nor
\(c>100/17\) is an asymptotic upper bound. The threshold table supplies no
growth law, no result for \(m>34\), no minimum over all polynomial selectors,
and no standard-complexity result for general factoring. The compact-step
count and the conservative bit-operation upper bound are separate quantities.

THM-002 has the same promise-versus-recognition boundary for a different,
odd \(p+1\)-asymmetry class. It requires a fresh exact uniform parameter at
every attempt, supplies no outside-promise termination or density guarantee,
and does not assert statistical independence from Pollard \(p-1\).

BAR-001 is a theorem about explicit divisibility signatures, not a lower bound
for every compressed batch representation or for the Umans--Wang
collision-resolution mechanism. Its order profile is factor dependent and is
used for analysis, not as an algorithmic input.

BAR-002 concerns only \(P=a+a^{-1}\). THM-002 shows that independent uniform
Lucas parameters do have a quantified success probability under DEF-007, but
it does not provide a universal construction. Neither result rules out
quadratic norm-one variants, elliptic-curve channels, adaptive selection, or
another parameter map.

BAR-003 concerns one common explicit finite exponent schedule and one uniform
distribution on unordered pairs from a declared finite prime set. Its
signatures require the unknown factors and therefore do not recognize promise
membership. The exact zero-density result requires its strict magnitude
hypothesis, while the asymptotic upper bound requires the displayed sparsity
hypothesis. It is neither a natural-density theorem nor a lower bound for
schedules whose polynomial-bit-length exponents have larger values.

BAR-004 extends the obstruction to polynomial-size explicit lists with
\(L(k)=o(k\log k)\), including \(O(k)\)-bit exponent values above the factors.
Its vanishing fraction is conditional on a stipulated exponentially large
finite prime population at common input length; the paper neither constructs
that population nor transfers the statement to natural semiprime density.
The result charges individual exponent encodings and evaluations. It does not
cover compressed or batched implicit families, adaptive factor-dependent
choices, \(L(k)\not=o(k\log k)\), exponentially many explicit exponents, or
other algebraic mechanisms, and is not a factoring lower bound.

BAR-005 closes one of those compressed cases only. It charges every node in a
factor-oblivious multiplication-only same-base DAG and tracks exact formal
exponents. It is not a lower bound for computing an equal residue at one fixed
modulus and base, for special endomorphisms, for adaptive factor-revealing
branches, or for another explicitly costed algebraic model.

BAR-006 resolves only a constant-sensitive part of the general
\(\Theta(k\log k)\)-node boundary before factor scale is imposed. BAR-007
closes every fixed coefficient for first-primes primorials, and BAR-008
extends that factor-scale conclusion to arbitrary squareful and
noninitial-support exponents in a common polynomial-size explicit schedule.
REF-007 shows that boundary node count alone is not sufficient. Longer
exponents, exponentially many exponents, adaptive schedules, and richer
representations remain outside BAR-008. None of these statements constructs
the population, recognizes the promise, or supplies a factoring lower bound.

BAR-009 closes only the explicitly charged same-base addition-subtraction
case on the unit branch after a base GCD precheck. It tracks exact formal
exponents and explicit outputs; it does not bound equal-residue computation
at a fixed modulus, implicit exponential batches, factor-dependent choices,
unrelated multi-base expressions, special endomorphisms, or other groups.
Direct proper factors found by the precheck are separate algorithmic exits,
not part of its factor-scale prime-signature count.

BAR-010 closes only selector-described standard product trees that enumerate,
evaluate, store, and charge every selected leaf. Its exact valuation identity
and root-to-leaf implication do not bound a specialized arithmetic circuit
that computes the same formal product without leaf materialization. The
BAR-008 transfer separately requires \(O(k\log k)\)-bit exponents, a common
factorization-independent schedule, fixed factor scale, and a stipulated
population. Reducing GCD calls does not reduce the charged leaf construction.

BAR-011 closes one genuinely non-materializing case: explicit atom residues
may feed a product-only DAG with shared subproducts. The theorem counts
formal repetitions exactly and shows that a proper node success already
occurs at a used atom. It does not cover addition, subtraction, division,
composition, closed-form atom synthesis, modulus-specific identities,
adaptive factor-dependent computation, unrelated bases, other groups, or a
general arithmetic-circuit model. Its BAR-008 transfer again needs a common
factorization-independent atom list with \(O(k\log k)\)-bit exponents.

BAR-012 closes only the dyadic identity
\((X^{2^t}-1)/(X-1)=\prod_{j<t}(X^{2^j}+1)\). It gives total semantics to
unit, proper, and full denominator GCDs and separates compact monomial
metadata from expanded output. It preserves the existence of a proper
component success, not the exact factor value returned by aggregation.

BAR-013 closes one arbitrary-exponent geometric sum. Its binary circuit has
polynomial compact cost in the charged base, modulus, and exponent lengths,
but its quotient success reduces to the endpoint in the unit branch, an
already proper denominator in the proper branch, or the public exponent GCD
in the full branch. The middle branch does not preserve the exact divisor
value.

BAR-014 closes the exact two-stage identity
\(S_{AB}(X)=S_A(X)S_B(X^A)\). Both the intermediate rational denominator and
the composed denominator have total semantics, and the proof charges compact,
dense, and sparse outputs separately. It does not cover iterated quotient
chains, unrelated cancellation certificates, arbitrary rational programs,
adaptive factor dependence, other groups, or general arithmetic-circuit lower
bounds.

BAR-015 closes only a nonempty public factor chain whose geometric quotient
at every stage is explicit. It retains all prefix and composed denominator
exits and proves a product-aggregate implication by success existence, not
identical divisor value. It does not cover cross-stage addition or
subtraction, arbitrary subset interfaces, unrelated denominators, adaptive
factor-dependent computation, other groups, or general rational/arithmetic
circuits.

BAR-016 shows that this last product implication is not stable under signed
addition. It proves a genuine component-to-aggregate separation in the exact
DEF-021 model, but provides neither a factorization-independent family that
succeeds on all composites nor a density, probability, or recognition
theorem. Adaptive coefficients, several interacting aggregates, unrelated
bases, other groups, and general arithmetic circuits remain outside.

BAR-017 classifies only the symmetric depth-two specialization
\((A_1,A_2)=(A,A)\), \((c_1,c_2)=(-1,1)\). Its endpoint/cofactor
factorization and valuation sum do not imply that either factor separates
every composite, and its compact cofactor evaluator exposes one aggregate
value rather than a hidden batch. Unequal factors, other coefficient pairs,
longer chains, adaptive choices, and general signed arithmetic circuits
remain outside.

BAR-018 treats one unequal depth-two chain and arbitrary nonzero public
coefficients. Its first-prefix trichotomy is total, and its normalized
difference has an exact common-step factor and endpoint polynomial-GCD
classification.

BAR-019 normalizes coefficient content and classifies overlap of the
aggregate with either charged stage by exact public resultants. Its
root-of-unity equation is exact, and its \(\Phi_4\) witness motivates
THM-003. THM-003 closes the rational-order list for this one unequal
depth-two numerator and gives requested-order recognition. BAR-020 then
proves compact evaluation and total valuation/extraction semantics for the
two exceptional cofactors, while REF-022 shows that their residual paths
strictly exceed the direct small-cyclotomic GCDs. BAR-021 then isolates all
stage and direct-cyclotomic overlap and rules out universal coverage by a
fixed finite joint base/parameter schedule. BAR-022 treats one
length-indexed extension only when exact integer lifts or equivalent explicit
support certificates are materialized. It gives an exact balanced-pair
coverage formula and necessary bit budget, but REF-024 proves that compact
modular evaluation does not imply that materialized budget. BAR-023 then
classifies one compact lift directly and proves that its one-bit support cut
cannot separate every pair, even if its support is broad. BAR-024 gives the
exact several-candidate injectivity criterion, candidate lower bounds, and
collision minimum. THM-004 constructs one diversified selector only on the
complete finite promise range \(9\le m\le15\), and BAR-025 proves that the
same selector already collides at \(m=16\). THM-005 widens the public box and
extends the complete finite construction through \(m=20\); BAR-026 gives the
exact five new thresholds and excludes the multiplicative endpoint
\(c=3/2\). BAR-027 then proves that both fixed M32 formulas collide at
\(m=21\), while THM-006 repairs that one new length with \(m+12\) and moves
the strict endpoint to \(32/21\). BAR-028 proves that both repaired formulas
collide at \(m=22\) and that caps 35 through 38 still fail, while THM-007
repairs the finite range with \(m+17\) and moves the strict endpoint to
\(19/11\). BAR-029 proves that both M34 formulas collide at \(m=23\), while
THM-008 repairs the finite range with \(m+24\) and moves the strict endpoint
to \(2\). BAR-030 separately refutes the two distinct M35 caps at \(m=24\),
while THM-009 repairs the finite range with \(m+27\) and moves the strict
endpoint to \(25/12\). BAR-031 refutes both distinct M36 caps at \(m=25\)
and preserves a collision through cap 64, while THM-010 repairs the finite
range with \(m+40\) and moves the strict endpoint to \(64/25\). BAR-032
refutes both distinct M37 caps at \(m=26\) and preserves a collision through
cap 70, while THM-011 repairs the finite range with \(m+45\) and moves the
strict endpoint to \(35/13\). BAR-033 refutes both distinct M38 caps at
\(m=27\) and preserves a collision through cap 86, while THM-012 repairs the
finite range with \(m+60\) and moves the strict endpoint to \(86/27\).
BAR-034 refutes both distinct M39 caps at \(m=28\) and preserves a collision
through cap 103, while THM-013 repairs the finite range with \(m+76\) and
moves the strict endpoint to \(103/28\). BAR-035 proves that both M40 caps
are injective at \(m=29\), identifies the smaller exact threshold 103, and
shows that both finite envelopes remain controlled by length 28. REF-037
records \(L_{29}^{\star}<L_{28}^{\star}\), refuting monotonicity across these
two different complete populations without implying a trend. BAR-036 then
refutes both inherited schedules at \(m=30\), preserves a final triple through
cap 122, and repairs the finite envelopes with \(m+93\) and \(c>61/15\).
BAR-037 again refutes both inherited schedules at \(m=31\), preserves the
last pair through cap 143, and repairs the finite envelopes with \(m+113\)
and \(c>143/31\). BAR-038 refutes both inherited schedules at \(m=32\),
preserves the last pair through cap 166, and repairs the finite envelopes
with \(m+135\) and \(c>83/16\). BAR-039 refutes both inherited schedules
at \(m=33\), preserves the last pair through cap 194, and repairs the
finite envelopes with \(m+162\) and \(c>194/33\). BAR-040 refutes both
inherited schedules at \(m=34\), preserves one pair at cap 200, and repairs
the finite envelopes at cap 201 with \(m+167\) and \(c>100/17\). BAR-041
then proves that every polynomial numeric DEF-032 cap eventually fails; the
finite table is not extrapolated to obtain this conclusion. The result does
not identify the first failure of an arbitrary cap, prove that a threshold
exists at every later length, or cover an \(N\)-dependent or adaptive
schedule, exponentially large encoded parameters, another compact grammar,
another promise, or general signed arithmetic circuits.

BAR-042 handles the encoded compact-gap family below the half-length span,
and BAR-043 extends the same exact family to every fixed linear span by
charging high-weight signatures to GCD-gap overlap integers. Neither result
covers superlinear span, other encoded bases or compact grammars,
\(N\)-dependent or adaptive schedules, or arbitrary circuits. The bounded
EXP-0048 profiles validate the implementation but do not establish the
asymptotic theorem.

The authoritative Rust layer is bounded to \texttt{u64}; validation primality
uses trial division and is not itself the polynomial-time procedure imported
in EXT-001. The literature set is sufficient for the technical statements
used here but is not a comprehensive current-state-of-the-art survey.

The main open problems are the two explicit universal constructor questions
in OPEN-002 and OPEN-003, recognition of the combined hereditary promise
without access to the factorization, whether a non-primorial boundary family
outside BAR-008's explicit-list model has a proved factor-scale
\(d\pm1\) yield guarantee, and whether a precisely defined specialized
arithmetic circuit, without the DEF-015 leaf-materialization requirement, can
evaluate a sufficiently rich formal batch at polynomial total construction,
evaluation, and extraction cost. For the compact-gap line, the remaining
boundary begins at superlinear level span; no next milestone is started here,
because work is paused after M49.

\section{Conclusion}

MOSEF remains a falsifiable research program, not a completed general
factoring algorithm. Its durable contributions are exact order-separation
semantics, support-signature injectivity and collision counts, restricted
promise theorems, scoped barriers for explicitly charged selector and circuit
families, and reproducible finite certificates.

For the public \(\mathcal T_{m,L}\) family, the strongest positive selector
statement is the computer-assisted finite promise theorem on balanced
square-free semiprimes for \(9\le m\le34\). The integrated table gives the
family-relative thresholds, predecessor collision buckets, and certified
incremental repairs. It establishes neither injectivity for \(m>34\) nor an
asymptotic cap law. The compact evaluator uses
\(O(L^3\log L)\) modular steps, while a conservative standard
bit-operation upper bound is \(O(L^3m^2\log L)\); offline population and
certificate generation are separate proof costs.

The negative results explain why finite success is not a general
breakthrough. BAR-041 rules out every polynomial numeric cap in the exact
DEF-032 grammar, BAR-042 rules out short-span encoded compact-gap lists, and
BAR-043 rules out every fixed linear span in that exact compact family. These
are not lower bounds for all polynomial selectors or for classical factoring.
Superlinear encoded lists, other compact grammars, adaptive public
constructions, promise recognition, and broader group channels remain open
research targets.


\appendix

\section{Full proofs of the separator criteria}

\subsection{Proof of LEM-001}\label{proof:LEM-001}

Choose \(p\in D_N(g,d)\). By the definition of multiplicative order,
\(g^d\equiv1\pmod p\), so \(p\mid\gcd(g^d-1,N)\) and the GCD exceeds one.
Because the support is proper, choose
\(q\in P(N)\setminus D_N(g,d)\). Then
\(g^d\not\equiv1\pmod q\), so \(q\) does not divide the GCD. Since \(q\mid N\),
the GCD is strictly smaller than \(N\). Repeated prime factors affect neither
strict inequality. \(\square\)

\subsection{Proof of LEM-002}\label{proof:LEM-002}

For each prime \(p\), the exponent of \(p\) in a GCD is the minimum of its
exponents in the two arguments. Its exponent in \(N\) is \(e_p\), while its
exponent in \(g^d-1\) is \(\nu_p(g^d-1)\), including the convention for zero.
Unique factorization gives
\[
\gcd(g^d-1,N)=
\prod_{p\in P(N)}p^{\min(e_p,\nu_p(g^d-1))}.
\]
The product is one exactly when every capped exponent is zero, and it is \(N\)
exactly when every capped exponent is \(e_p\). These are the only trivial
GCDs.

If \(N\) is square-free, every \(e_p=1\), and
\[
a_p=1
\Longleftrightarrow p\mid g^d-1
\Longleftrightarrow\operatorname{ord}_p(g)\mid d.
\]
Thus support and valuation separation coincide. If \(N=p^e\), the support can
never be proper, while the valuation criterion is precisely
\(0<\nu_p(g^d-1)<e\). \(\square\)

\section{Full proof of the restricted theorem}

\subsection{Proof of THM-001}\label{proof:THM-001}

\paragraph{Algorithm.}
For a reached composite non-perfect-power \(K\), put
\(k=\lceil\log_2K\rceil\) and
\(M=M_B(k)\). Repeat complete cycles over \(t=1,\ldots,R(k)\). At every
\(t\), sample a fresh exact uniform \(a\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}\), return
\(\gcd(a,K)\) if it is proper, and otherwise return
\(\gcd(a^{tM}-1,K)\) if that GCD is proper.

The complete factorer first runs deterministic polynomial-time primality
testing. It next performs exact maximal-exponent perfect-power detection. If
\(K=b^e\), it recursively factors \(b\) and multiplies every returned
multiplicity by \(e\). At every other composite, it invokes the randomized
splitter, validates the returned divisor, and recursively factors both
children.

Exact uniform sampling uses rejection: draw \(k\) unbiased bits as
\(x\in[0,2^k-1]\) and reject \(x\ge K\). Since
\(2^{k-1}<K\le2^k\), acceptance probability exceeds \(1/2\), so fewer than two
\(k\)-bit draws are needed in expectation.

\paragraph{One-trial success.}
Fix a promise witness \((p,q,t)\) and let \(d=tM_B(k)\). If
\(1<\gcd(a,K)<K\), the first GCD succeeds. The only residue whose GCD with
\(K\) is \(K\) is \(a=0\).

For a unit \(a\), Fermat's theorem and \(p-1\mid d\) give
\(a^d\equiv1\pmod p\). Projection of a uniform unit modulo \(K\) to
\((\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z)^\times\) is uniform. This cyclic group has order
\(q-1\), so \(x^d=1\) has exactly \(\gcd(d,q-1)\) roots. Because
\(q-1\nmid d\), that proper divisor of \(q-1\) is at most \((q-1)/2\).
At least half the units therefore satisfy \(a^d\not\equiv1\pmod q\), and each
such unit separates \(p\) from \(q\).

There are \(K-\varphi(K)-1\) proper nonunit residues and at least
\(\varphi(K)/2\) successful units. Hence
\[
\Pr[\mathrm{success}]
\ge\frac{K-1-\varphi(K)/2}{K}
\ge\frac{K-1}{2K}.
\]
A composite non-perfect-power has at least two distinct prime divisors, so
\(K\ge6\), and the final quantity is at least \(5/12\).

\paragraph{Correctness and termination.}
Every returned split is explicitly checked to lie strictly between \(1\) and
the current input. Prime leaves are recognized deterministically, and exact
perfect-power expansion preserves multiplicities. The algorithm therefore
never returns an incorrect factorization.

One value of \(t\) in each cycle is a witness. Conditional on any prior
